# Funding Proposal

## Version 1.1

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is seeking high-quality funding proposals. Accredited entities are expected to develop their funding proposals in close consultation with the relevant national designated authority, with due consideration of the GCF's investment framework. The funding proposals should demonstrate how the proposed investment criteria and climate resilience of subsistence farmers and undershift communities residing in the vulnerable catchment of Sri Lanka will be achieved.

## Project Programme Title: Agricultural Plantation for Livelihoods in the Mountain Ranges

Country/Region: Sri Lanka

Accredited Entity: IUCN

Initial submission: May, 13th September 2020

Date of Submission: 22 January 2020.

     1. The proposed project will enhance the ability of communities to address climate-induced shortages of inputs 
     and lowlands. Climate smart projects investments in agriulture and value-added livelihoods in the area with 
     the risks related to increased temperatures, changes in precipitation patterns and events that cause extended droughts. There will 
     be a focus on improving the resilience of smallholder farmers, plantation operations and the

     and develop replicable financial models, electronic trading platforms for livestock and fisheries products; and the

     Please provide a brief description of the proposed project benefits (see investment criteria in section E). The details 
     1. The proposed project will enhance the ability of smallholder subsistence farmers to address climate-induced shortages of irrigation and lowlands. Climate smart projects investments in agroculture and value-added livelihoods in the area with 
     the risks related to increased temperatures, changes in precipitation patterns and other extreme weather events that cause extended droughts. The project will 
     adaptation information portals and advisory services, improve access to agricultural water supply and affordable 
     planning as a resilience model. The project will also develop replicable financial models, electronic trans- 
     mission, and other innovative solutions for the development of sustainable livelihoods. 
     2. Key project activities will comprise: participatory goal-setting, adaptation information portals and advisory services, 
     planning as a resilience model, development of replicable financial models, electronic trans- 
     mission, and other innovative solutions for the development of sustainable livelihoods. The project will also develop 
     measurable benefits which will include: i) 1.3 million registered natural ecosystems protected and strengthened in resilience; ii) 346,000 hectares of 
     improved access to agricultural water supply and affordable planning as a resilience model; iii) 250,000 hectares of 
     sustainable livelihoods in the area with the highest risk of increased temperatures, changes in 
     precipitation patterns and other climate-related events that cause extended droughts. The project will also 
     develop a national action plan for the implementation of the proposed project.

     ration. The national water collection and delivery systems is

 | According to Bastiaansen, in June to June in 1990 to 2000, the project area has changed due to poor land use. The harvested by the highland tandiscapes are affected due to high intensity rainfall and low groundwater and enhance surface water | 
 | Intense rains in the harvesting season in 2016 destroyed the harvest of mature rice in fields. | 
 | Cloud interception or canopy closure due to intensive rainfall can be mitigated by the extent of rainfall evaporation from rainfall falls between 5 mm areas in the upper monsoon regions. | 
 | North-East monsoons and landforms in the form of ridges, hills and mountains can also reduce the intensity of rainfall. |

<doc> Detailed deliverables and millstones of the proposed project 
 M1 - Protected streams and well managed drainage system for runoff water 
 M2 - Rehabilitation of tanks and other RWH methods established 
 M3 - A list / data base of business cases that required performance base flow 
 M4 - Restoration of forest cover degraded 
 M5 - PES established under a multi stakeholder platform 
 M6 - Monitoring system is established. 
 M7 - Development of integrated land use policy and planning mechanism 
 M8 - Systems developed to improve land and water management in upstream services 
 M9 - Payment for environmental services is developed 
 M10 - Options are available for SLM and sustainable intensification 
 M11 - Mechanism to incorporate governance in SLM and productivity enhancement 
 M12 - Development of a system to support land use planning, climate adaptation and market information 
 M13 - High intensity of small holder production in downstream and upstream 
 M14 - Restored plantations and sustainable intensification 
 D4 - Sustainable production by the small holder farmers in upstream areas 
 M15 - All guidelines and tools for adaptation are developed 
 D5 - Rural advisory system is improved to respond climate, development, 
 D6 - More efficient green value chains and new markets for small holder far- 
 M16 - Implementation completion of the GCF project 
 M17 - Implementation of the GCF project 
 M18 - Inception Report 
 M19-M24- Annual Performance Reports 
 M25-M30- Mid-term Evaluation 
 D8 - End-of-Term Evaluation </doc>

<doc> This activity focuses on the development of smart farming varieties and makes efficient use of irrigation water 
 recycling as well as employing integrated pest control (connected to the information system developed in 1.2.1). 
 Because traditional irrigation methods like furrow irrigation (FI) and continuous flowing irrigation (CFI) result 
 in high water loss, the project will promote water-saving techniques such as drip irrigation (DFI), subsurface drip 
 irrigation (SDI) and mulch irrigation. A main vehicle will be to support women’s active participation in the design, development and 
 implementation of farming systems that are based on sustainable practices. The project will also support women’s involvement 
 in tree species selection and planting. Examples are the consideration of women’s economic position as strengthening their 
 preferences for trees that can be harvested for non-food purposes. 
 1.2.1: Increasing cropping intensity of irrigated areas 
 This activity focusses on the development of smart farming varieties and makes efficient use of irrigation water recycling as well as employing integrated pest control (connected to the information system developed in 1.2.1). 
 Because traditional irrigation methods like furrow irrigation (FI) and continuous flowing irrigation (CFI) result 
 in high water loss, the project will promote water-saving techniques such as drip irrigation (DWI) and subsurface drip irrigation (SSDI). The project 
 will also support women’s involvement in tree species selection and planting. Examples are the consideration of women’s economic position as strengthening their 
 preferences for trees that can be harvested for non-food purposes. 
 1.2.2: Intensification of Sustainable smallholder production 
 This activity will focus on options for sustainable smallholder production and control the distribution of tree species in multi-strata systems. The project 
 aims to improve the quality and agronomic management of home gardens, analog forests, spice plantations and other crops in the upstream 
 areas. Home gardens are species-rich, tree-based systems that are adaptable to the household and are used to produce a diverse array of foods, trees and other products from 
 tropical trees. The project will further enhance the productivity and business skills of farmers by supporting them in 
 enhancing rice productivity through use of appropriate maintaining quality as well as supporting the enhanced resiliency. 
 This activity focuses on the development of smart irrigation varieties and makes efficient use of irrigation water, recycling as well as employing integrated pest control (IPCC)

<doc> The PMU and gender specialist will provide gender specific training to the relevant executing agencies' 
 project teams in order these are fully versed in gender-sensitivity and inclusivity and how to reflect this in 
 their respective work packages. 
 An amount of USD 200,000 has been allocated for supporting the implementation of the GAP. As such it 
 will cover the costs for the gender specialist, for activities that will be identified through the above 
 described in the Gender Action Plan below. In order to monitor the effectiveness of how the gender-aspects

 will be addressed in each of the project activities will be 
 in empowering women in the management of land,
